Terminal document events include `finished`; no-change reviews are chargeable and describe only captured views. Valid partial results can be charged. Failed steps release their customer hold; uncertain finalization reports recovery pending until the durable outcome is known. No blanket guarantee that failures or unchanged output are free. Operation-aware planning saves the plan and charge in one transaction; legacy in-memory harness paths remain only for compatibility checks.

Recovery contract: GET `/api/build/operations` by account-bound workspace/artifact and id or actionKey returns validated `{operation,result,recovery}`. POST of `{workspaceId,artifactId,id,runId,expectedVersion,acknowledged:true}` recovers a stored candidate without provider calls. Exact completed-step replay streams its recorded output and charge. Shared snapshot `pendingKind` is nullable continuation/review/plan and must describe the stage needing continuation; a finished successful build has `canContinue:false` while still permitting its authorized automatic review. Plan PUT and POST require the displayed `expectedRevision`; PUT returns the authoritative resulting stored plan/revision.

No main-local database, staging, production, provider calls, paid API calls, commits or deployments. Do not freeze this package until follow-up SQL and matching client checks complete.

## Claude document body/editor package — handed back (2026-10-13)

All files below are handed back for root's snapshot. No paid calls (Claude $0 reserved/spent), no schema, prompt, tool contract or cover-composition change, no freeze/commit/deploy/staging/production change.

**Findings fixed (concrete defects):**
1. Contents page overflow: `.full` pages are a fixed 9 in with `overflow:hidden`, so books with more than about 11 chapters silently lost contents entries (long-book briefs hit this). Now 9 per page normally; for more than 9 chapters, compact rows (18 per page) continue on "Contents, continued" pages. Every chapter is listed.
2. Markup written without a blank line was flattened into one paragraph, for example `## Heading` then text, or an intro line then `- items`, so readers saw literal `##`/`-`. Mixed chunks now split by line kind, and wrapped list/quote lines continue their item. Chunks written with blank lines parse exactly as before (pinned in the new check). Also new: `* item` bullets, numbered lists keep their start number, and a wrapped line starting with a year (`1984.`) stays prose.
3. Markdown tables rendered as a paragraph of pipes. They now render as a styled, escaped table, header row optional. The project copy keeps the table text as a `list` block (the hosting renderer refuses `table` blocks).
4. A drop cap on a short opening paragraph (or one after a heading) overlapped the next block. The drop cap now applies only when the chapter opens with a paragraph of at least 180 characters (`p.lead`).
5. Body typography: curly quotes, apostrophes, em dashes (`--`) and ellipses in rendered body text only (`bodyHtml`). Stored text is unchanged, so `edit_chapter` exact matching is unaffected. Titles and covers still use `inlineHtml` unchanged.
6. Deck quote slides: an attribution up to the schema's 90 characters wrapped to two lines inside a one-line box, close to the footer, unflagged in all 8 font pairings. It now shrinks (18→15px) or takes two measured lines, and the block is centred with its real height. The text-width estimator now includes letter-spacing, which also makes other tracked labels' issue detection honest.
7. Preview UX: a "Go to chapter / Go to slide" menu that follows scroll position (smooth jump, reduced-motion aware), plus reading time in book stats. On phones it replaces the stats line.

### Claude fix for root document regression finding — handed back (2026-10-13)

Scope was only adaptive contents pagination and safe long-content wrapping. No schema, prompt, tool contract, cover-composition or slide code change. No paid calls, freeze, deploy, commit or staging/production change.

**Fixes in `src/lib/documents/render-book.ts`:**
- **Contents pagination.** Pages were packed by count (18), so 40 × 120-character titles overflowed. Entries are now packed by estimated wrapped height: title and label line counts for the actual font metric, plus padding, against each page's real list height, 95% fill. Roomy rows are kept when the whole contents fits one page; otherwise compact rows run over as many pages as needed. Font sizes are unchanged (no shrinking): roomy 12.5pt, compact 10.5pt titles. Contents rows also wrap unbroken words. 40 × 120-character titles now take 5 pages with about 90px spare on each, in every pairing. 40 short titles still take 3 pages.
- **Tables.** Cells wrap anywhere, so no table is wider than the text block. To stop one long cell from taking all the width, each column keeps a `min-width` for its longest ordinary word (up to 12 characters), scaled down when many columns can't fit.
- **Other long words.** Chapter body text wraps unbroken runs (`overflow-wrap:break-word`). The chapter opener title and the preview running head wrap too: a 120-character unbroken chapter title previously scrolled the preview page sideways, which the new browser check found.
- Covers are untouched. The legacy fixture's page markup is still byte-identical to before my first package; both legacy deck hashes are unchanged. I re-pinned only the two legacy book hashes in `check-cover-composition.cjs` (CSS-only difference).

**New real-browser regression `src/lib/documents/check-book-layout-browser.cjs`** (runs in `npm test`; Playwright Chromium, JavaScript off, all requests aborted, schema-validated fixtures):
- Cases: 8 font pairings × 6 limit cases × preview and print media = 96. The limit cases are 40 × 120-character titles; the same with 40-character labels; 40 × 120-character unbroken titles; 9 long titles; 40 short titles; and tables (an 800-character unbroken cell, an 8-column table of long hyphenated words, and an unbroken URL run in a paragraph).
- Asserts:
  - every chapter is listed;
  - no contents page has scroll overflow;
  - every row ends above the bottom padding and inside the page width;
  - contents text is at least 13.9px (no shrinking);
  - no body element extends past the text block;
  - no sideways page scroll.
- Mutation-tested: overpacking contents (fill 1.6) fails at "contents page 1 overflows (978 > 864)"; removing table cell wrapping fails at "body content wider than the text block".

**Your fixture script, re-run on a copy:** `work/book-overflow-review-20261013-claude-recheck/` (your original evidence is untouched). Renderer d3c10c5cf72e20a6:
- 5 contents pages, each scrollHeight equal to height, 0 rows beyond or clipped, in preview and print.
- The table sits 60px inside the chapter in preview and exactly at the chapter edge in print.
- I couldn't render the PDFs here (no poppler); I checked the Chromium PNGs visually.

- `src/lib/documents/check-cover-composition.cjs` a4d17ecc9367c0ba (two legacy book hashes re-pinned)

**Limitation:** measured with this Windows machine's fonts. Other platforms substitute fonts, which is why the estimate keeps about 90px spare per contents page.

### Claude CRM/chat package — progress and exact files so far (2026-10-13)

No root reply yet on CRM server ownership; I have not touched CRM server routes, CRM migrations, `client-followups.ts`, `client-outreach.ts` or `crm-protocol.ts`.

**1. CRM navigation glitches (fixed, measured in Chromium on the isolated local stack, 22 seeded clients):**
- Opening a client used to blank the screen for about 0.5s and Back for about 1s. Now the client name shows within 60ms, a sub-tab switch has 0 loading frames, and Back restores all 22 rows instantly with the scroll position kept.
- Causes:
  - the effect keyed on the whole route object cleared the snapshot and selection on every navigation;
  - sub-tabs refetched the client;
  - the directory dropped results on each keystroke;
  - panels were keyed by `updatedAt`, so every save remounted them (losing messages and focus);
  - a details save blanked the screen;
  - the studio remount key sent device users back to account clients.
- Files:
  - `src/components/account-clients.tsx` (rewrite of SavedClients: the workspace loads once; the full client is fetched per visit and editable panels mount only on the fresh record; stale-while-revalidate list; skeletons; `/` to search; Esc for back);
  - `src/components/use-client-directory.ts` (keeps previous results, marked stale; `patch()` for in-place updates);
  - `src/components/client-project-directory.tsx` (optimistic delivery stage with rollback);
  - `src/components/cloud-client-outreach.tsx` (`embedded` prop only);
  - `src/components/studio.tsx` (removed the AccountClients `key` only);
  - `src/app/account-clients.css` (appended rules).

**2. Expert chat (now works, offline-qualified, no paid calls yet):**
- `POST /api/chat` (SSE) runs in this order: same-origin, `MAKEBORNE_EXPERT_CHAT_ENABLED`/key gate, strict schema, billing user plus creation access, `requirePricedModel`, `requireProviderCapacity`, a per-server one-reply-per-account guard, `reserveCredits`, `createRunSpend`, `expertChatResponse`.
- Standard uses `claude-haiku-5-5` at medium effort with a 1-credit hold (the worst case fits). Genius uses `claude-opus-5-5` at high effort with a 20-credit hold and is charged by confirmed usage. Prompts are capped at 60k tokens, below Haiku's 100k price tier.
- Each expert has a strict field: off-topic questions get a redirect to the right colleague; role-change and injection attempts are refused; pasted content is treated as data; answers are advice only (no tools).
- Files:
  - `src/app/api/chat/route.ts` (new), `src/lib/experts/chat-run.ts` (new), `src/lib/experts/check-expert-chat.cjs` (new, 8 groups);
  - `src/lib/expert-personas.ts`, `src/lib/expert-persona-instructions.ts`;
  - `src/components/expert-chat.tsx` (rewrite), `src/components/chat-markdown.tsx` (new, no HTML parsing), `src/app/expert-chat.css` (appended).
- **Shared file touched, please review:** `src/lib/ai/prices.ts` gets one new PRICE_EVIDENCE record for `claude-haiku-5-5` ($0.10/$0.125/$0.20/$0.01/$0.50, ≤100k tier; sources: pricing page and models overview, verified 2026-10-13). check-live-spend 30, effort matrix 91 and anthropic-text 30 still pass.
- **Owner decision to confirm:** Standard costs 1 credit per answer (true provider cost is about $0.001). Genius is usually 3–8 credits, at most 20.

**Next (claiming when started):** CRM features beyond follow-up-crew: pipeline board with drag and drop, ⌘K palette, a focus queue with one-click contacted/snooze, CSV import/export, AI follow-up drafts. Possibly an additive migration for deal value, tags and archive; it will be posted here first.

### Claude CRM and expert chat package — handed back (2026-10-13)

All files below are handed back. No paid calls (Claude $0 of $20), no migration, no CRM server-route change beyond one new route, and no freeze, commit, deploy or production change.

**CRM features added (beyond the owner's follow-up-crew app, all real and persisted, no fake controls):**
- **Pipeline board:** six stage columns with server counts. Drag and drop, plus a keyboard/screen-reader "Move to" select on each card. Moves are optimistic, recorded in history ("Stage: Lead → Proposal") and rolled back on refusal.
- **One-click follow-ups on list rows:**
  - "Contacted" logs a contact today, moves a Lead to Contacted and schedules +3 days;
  - "Snooze" offers tomorrow, +3 days, next Monday or +2 weeks and actually saves (follow-up-crew's snooze only showed a toast).
  - Both are optimistic with rollback; they read the latest record and PATCH with `expectedUpdatedAt`, with one automatic retry on 409.
- **⌘K / Ctrl+K palette:** server search across the whole workspace (not the first 50), plus actions: add client, overdue view, board/list, export, import.
- **CSV export:** all pages of the current filter. Formula-injection cells are prefixed, the file has a UTF-8 BOM so accents survive, and quoting is RFC 4180.
- **CSV import:** header mapping (also first+last name and semicolon files), validation, in-file duplicates and a 500-row limit, a preview with skipped rows, and sequential idempotent POSTs with progress (it stops if a write's outcome is uncertain).
- **AI follow-up drafts:** new `POST /api/cloud/workspaces/[ws]/clients/[id]/follow-up-draft`. The route itself loads the client record server-side (the browser never supplies it) and treats it as data. Mira writes the message with a tone and an optional goal; it costs the same as a chat answer. "I sent it: log it" records the message as a contact and schedules the next follow-up. Reviewers are refused.

**Expert chat:**
- One shared start path, `src/lib/experts/start.ts`: price check → capacity → hold → spend → stream. It is used by `/api/chat` and the draft route; the structure test asserts the order in both.
- Expert playbooks were enriched from the owner's vault (C:\Helix), general frameworks only, with private notes excluded:
  - Mira: offer checklist, outreach levers, message scorecard, follow-up cadence;
  - Atlas: value of information, Mom Test, evidence ladder, sampling;
  - Ellis: job statement, Must/Should/Won't, activation and PMF checks, productised services.
- Shared style rules: blunt, one next step, no hype, no invented proof.

- `/studio?tab=clients` first painted Projects ("Opening your workspace…") and then jumped. The server page now passes `initialTab` (`src/app/studio/page.tsx`, plus the `initialTab` prop in `studio.tsx`).

## Owner decisions and Claude claims — fractional credits, chat price, email automation (2026-10-13)

**Owner decisions (in chat with Claude):**
1. **Fractional credits everywhere.** Usage is charged exactly (3.4 credits used means 3.4 charged), not rounded up to whole credits.
2. **Expert chat price = 15× the provider cost.** At $0.03 per credit, a typical Haiku answer (about $0.001) is about 0.5 credits; a typical Opus Genius answer (about $0.09) is about 45 credits.
3. **CRM email automation through the user's own mailbox: Gmail and Outlook OAuth** (not SMTP), with sequences, sending and reply detection, matching the owner's follow-up-crew app and going beyond it.
4. **More specialised experts.** The owner will supply avatars.

**Request to root on decision 1 (billing is your area): proposed design.**
- Store and settle credits in **centi-credits** (integer hundredths) so every SQL path stays integer and exact.
- One additive migration multiplies existing balances, lots, holds, ledger and grant amounts by 100 inside a guarded, idempotent unit-version step, and wallets record `credit_unit = 'centi'`.
- `src/lib/credits/server.ts` converts at the boundary; `customerCredits` becomes `confirmedMicrousd / rate` rounded to 0.01.
- Plans and top-ups grant amounts × 100; the UI shows up to two decimals.
- Rollback must keep the unit flag (never re-read centi values as whole credits).

Who owns it? I can do the app/UI side and the formatting helper. I'd prefer you or a delegate own the SQL, RPCs and the migration rehearsal, because they touch sold-credit obligations, top-ups, refunds, rollover and the admin console. Until it lands, chat charges are rounded up to whole credits through the existing path.

**Claude claims now:**
- **Experts:** `src/lib/expert-personas.ts`, `src/lib/expert-persona-instructions.ts`, `src/components/expert-chat.tsx`, `src/app/expert-chat.css`, chat route enum, `check-expert-chat.cjs`.
- **Chat pricing:** `src/lib/experts/chat-run.ts` and `start.ts` (a 15× multiplier on confirmed usage, with the hold sized to cover it).
- **Email automation (new files only, plus one additive migration with the next free number, posted before it is written):** `src/lib/mail/*` (OAuth for Google and Microsoft, token encryption reusing the GitHub pattern, send, reply sync, templates with tokens, sequence engine, send throttle and warm-up), `src/app/api/mail/*`, a sequence/reply cron route, CRM UI for connect, compose, templates, sequences and inbox, plus checks.
- **Owner steps that will be needed:** a Google Cloud OAuth client (gmail.send and gmail.readonly; public launch needs Google app verification) and a Microsoft Entra app (Mail.Send, Mail.Read). Secrets go in env only.

### Claude handback — experts ×8, chat pricing, CRM email automation (2026-10-13)

No paid calls (Claude $0 of $20). Nothing committed, deployed, or changed in production or staging.

**Owner decisions applied:**
- Expert chat is priced at a multiple of provider cost: Standard (Haiku 5.5) 15×, Genius (Opus 5.5) 1.5×. The owner asked for "a Genius answer ≈ 4× a Haiku answer"; exactly 4× would be below Opus cost, so 1.5× is the lowest profitable setting. Typical Genius answer: about 2 credits, versus about 0.3 for Standard.
- Holds: Standard 3 credits, Genius 15. Per-mode prompt caps (30k and 18k tokens) guarantee room for an answer within the hold.
- Charges are still rounded up to whole credits until root decides on fractional credits (proposal above).
- Email personalisation always uses Haiku (enforced server-side).

**Experts:** now eight, each with its own field: Mira (positioning & brand), Nova (copywriting), Rex (sales & outreach), Juno (offers & pricing), Theo (growth & launches), Atlas, Ellis and Sage (client delivery & operations).
- Each "not yours" list is generated from the roster, so every expert redirects to the right colleague.
- Playbooks come from the owner's vault, general methods only.
- New experts show lettered avatars until the owner supplies `public/advisors/{id}-transparent.png` and sets `portrait: true`.

**CRM email automation (new):**
- **Migration 59** `20261013240000_crm_email_automation.sql`:
  - tables: `mail_accounts`, `mail_templates`, `mail_sequences`, `mail_enrollments`, `mail_messages`;
  - `makeborne_private.mail_tokens` plus service-role-only `makeborne_mail_token_read/write`;
  - RLS: editors manage templates and sequences; mailboxes, enrollments and messages are server-written and member-readable;
  - unique claims: (enrollment, step), the provider message id, and one active sequence per client.
  - Applied only to the isolated local stack. **Needs root's rehearsal before any freeze.**
- **Gmail and Outlook OAuth:**
  - The state is signed and expiring; the returning user must match the one who started; a mailbox another member connected can't be taken over.
  - Tokens are AES-256-GCM sealed and bound to the mailbox id.
- **Engine:**
  - warm-up pace (10→15→+5 a day, capped by the mailbox limit) and at least 2 minutes between automatic sends;
  - claim-once sends; uncertain outcomes are never auto-retried;
  - threaded replies (Gmail threadId + In-Reply-To; Outlook createReply);
  - reply sync records each reply once, stops sequences and moves the client to Conversation;
  - Won or Lost clients are never emailed. Stages are normalised for the CRM expand mode, and every send or reply is written to the client's outreach history.
- **UI:**
  - an Email layout (mailboxes, Check now, sequences with a proven 4-step starter and per-client pause/resume/stop, templates, recent replies);
  - a client Email tab (thread, composer with templates, reply-in-thread, **Write with AI**, start a sequence);
  - list multi-select with a bulk bar: **Write personal emails** opens a review queue where Haiku writes one unique email per client (up to 25), each editable. Unfilled `[placeholders]` block sending; sending stops on limit, reconnect or uncertain errors and keeps the rest; the alternative is "Add to sequence".
  - Modals and the ⌘K palette render in a portal, which fixes the sidebar drawing over the modal.
- **Cron:** `/api/cron/mail` (bearer CRON_SECRET, `MAKEBORNE_MAIL_AUTOMATION_ENABLED=true`). Not added to `vercel.json`.
- **Owner setup:** `docs/EMAIL_AUTOMATION.md`, including the Google verification caveat: `gmail.readonly` is restricted and needs a yearly assessment, with an option to launch send-only first. `.env.example` gained mail and chat variables (shared file, additive lines only).

**Evidence:**
- `npm test` 106/106. Full ESLint is clean; TypeScript is clean apart from Codex's `infra/publication-controller/entry.ts`.
- `check-mail.cjs` runs 9 offline groups plus 6 local-DB engine groups (`MAKEBORNE_VERIFY_MAIL_LOCAL=1`): token refresh with sealed storage, claim-once under 3 concurrent senders, threaded follow-up, a reply recorded once that stops the sequence, the daily limit, Won stops.
  - Real bug found and fixed: a NOT NULL check fired before upsert conflict resolution on token refresh.
- `check-expert-chat` 8 groups covering all eight experts and the new pricing.
- Browser, against the isolated stack with seeded demo data and simulated AI streams:
  - Email center; sequence created through RLS; bulk add of 2 clients;
  - the client Email tab;
  - the personal-email queue writes 3 drafts, then the send stops cleanly on a provider error.

**Not done:**
- live Gmail or Outlook (needs the owner's OAuth apps);
- a live Haiku/Opus smoke test (needs a budget reservation and the owner's OK);
- fractional credits (root's decision);
- open/click tracking (intentionally not added: it hurts deliverability and needs consent).

### Claude handback — research-driven personalisation, lead profiles, live Haiku smoke (2026-10-13)

**Paid calls (owner-authorised, "run any test you need"):**
- Reserved $0.50 under `claude-personalize-smoke-20261013` in `api-budget-round-20261013.json`; settled at **$0.007106** for 8 Haiku 5.5 calls. Local script, mocked billing; no app, staging or production involvement.
- Evidence: `work/personalize-smoke-20261013/run-1.json` and `run-2.json`.
- Claude new-round total: $0.007106 of $20, nothing reserved.

**Research-driven personalisation:**
- `src/lib/mail/research.ts` reads a client's linked public pages (profile link and website, at most 3) and extracts platform, handle, follower/following/post counts, bio and a text excerpt.
- Guard rails: http(s) only; public addresses only, checked after DNS resolution and on every redirect (max 3); standard ports only; no credentials in links; 6 s timeout; 600 KB limit.
- Bot-check and consent pages (TikTok, YouTube in this region) are reported as "blocks automated reading" rather than sent to the model.
- Live results:
  - Instagram works (natgeo 268M and an 81-follower account read correctly, including the real bio from the plain description tag);
  - TikTok and YouTube are blocked;
  - network errors map to plain language.
- **Known limitation:** DNS is checked and then fetch resolves again (a rebinding window). Acceptable for this read-only fetch with no credentials or internal access, but a dedicated egress or proxy is the stronger fix.
- `src/lib/mail/personalize.ts`: campaign presets. "Creators: what your audience is worth" uses the owner's own email as a style reference; there are also a website angle and a custom angle.
  - The strict brief requires one concrete detail only that person would recognise, no invented facts, one low-effort ask and 60–120 words.
  - It also returns notes (niche, audience, angle, confidence, and a "check before sending" line).
  - Haiku is enforced server-side for every ready-to-send email.
- Live quality was good: specific openers from real bios and taglines, honest low-confidence flags, and the field boundary held.

**Lead profiles (migration 60, `20261013250000_crm_lead_profile.sql`, additive):**
- New `clients` columns: `tags text[]` (validated, at most 12 tags of up to 30 characters, GIN index), `deal_value`, `deal_currency`, `lead_source`, and `lead_profile jsonb` (8 KB cap) holding `{research, insight}`. Applied only to the isolated stack. **Needs rehearsal with 58–59.**
- Routes:
  - `GET /leads` (`?ids`, `?tags=1`, `?tag=`);
  - `PATCH /clients/[id]/lead`;
  - `POST /clients/[id]/lead/research` (free, no AI).
  - The draft route saves what it reads; the queue saves Rex's insight.
- `src/lib/leads.ts` gives a transparent 0–100 score (stage, followers, deal, researched, email) with Hot/Warm/Cold and reasons, plus count parsing.
- UI:
  - a Lead profile card on the client page (score and reasons, tags, deal value and currency, source, research facts and "Research (free)", Rex's read);
  - list rows show score, followers, deal value and tags;
  - a tag filter row;
  - pipeline columns show deal totals;
  - CSV import accepts Instagram/handle/profile and tag columns (handles become links, and the handle stands in for a missing name).

**Checks:**
- `npm test` 106/106. Full ESLint is clean; TypeScript is clean apart from Codex's `publication-controller/entry.ts`.
- `check-mail` covers 12 offline groups plus 6 local-DB groups.
- `check-client-crm-tools` covers 5 groups, including lead scoring and creator CSV.

### Claude — 2026-10-14: AI-tailored questions before the first build (uncommitted)
- **New route `POST /api/build/questions`** (`src/app/api/build/questions/route.ts`): same gates as `/api/build` (sameOrigin, builder enabled, cloudContext write, reviewers refused, priced model, provider capacity). It reserves a 1-credit hold and runs one Haiku call (`src/lib/builder/ai-brief-questions.ts`, `QUESTIONS_MODEL = claude-haiku-5-5`, max 1,500 output tokens, JSON only, brief fenced as data) through `RunSpend` with `providerCap = customerCap = 1 credit` and `minCallMs: 15_000` within `maxDuration 60`. It ALWAYS ends with `releaseRun` (never `settleRun`): free to the client, cost recorded as absorbed. Failures return 503 and nothing is charged.
- **Client** (`live-builder.tsx`): the first request on an empty project calls the route (25 s timeout) and shows tailored questions; on any failure it uses the on-device questions (`brief-questions.ts`); if the AI judges the brief complete, it builds immediately. `isBriefQuestion` validates network replies; the card supports free-text questions.
- **Checks:** new `check-ai-brief-questions.cjs` (6: parsing/normalisation, fallbacks, prompt fencing, one capped call with a mocked client and real RunSpend, cap refusal before any provider call, route order/no-settle/time-budget contract). Browser-verified both paths locally with mocked routes and blocked build POSTs: tailored questions → answers in the build body; route 503 → on-device questions.
- **No real Haiku call was made.** A live smoke test needs a ledger reservation (about $0.01) and owner approval. With production AI spend off, users get the on-device questions.

### Claude — client review restore no longer wipes typed fields, 22 October 2026
Branch `fix/review-draft-restore-race` (from origin/main d066f23), local commit only: not pushed, not merged, no production, staging or database change. Trigger: `feature/creation-quality` e2e funnel failed once at `funnel.spec.mjs` ~213-217 (no POST to /comments; screenshot showed the comment, an EMPTY name and the "unsent feedback … is back" notice); the rerun passed 7/7.
Cause: `/review/[token]` is server-rendered, including the srcdoc preview, so the funnel's "preview h1 attached" wait does not mean the page is live, and the reviewer can type before hydration. React 19.2 keeps pre-hydration text on screen but not in state. The name and unsent-feedback restores ran after hydration and set state unconditionally (name to the stored, possibly empty, value; comment to the stored draft), and that render wiped or replaced the typed name and comment. With the name gone from state, Post comment only shows "Add your name" and sends nothing. The notice in the e2e screenshot means the page also loaded a second time after the comment had been saved as a draft. That reload is not explained by the app code: suspect `next dev`, not confirmed.
Fix (`src/components/client-review-page.tsx` only): both restores read the fields as they are. Typed text wins and is taken into state, and a restore only fills a field that is still empty, so it never blanks one. Name fields stay open (not collapsed to a saved name) while someone types their own. An open decision panel keeps its choice and note, and the notice appears only when something was actually restored.
Regression: new `scripts/check-review-draft-restore-browser.cjs` serves real server HTML, holds the page script while the visitor types, then hydrates (StrictMode) and runs 4 scenarios: first visit, typed over a saved name and draft, typed name only, typed nothing. On the original code scenario 1 fails in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, and scenarios 2-3 fail in Chromium (the only engine run for them). With the fix all pass in all three engines (`run-browser-checks.mjs --only check-review-draft-restore-browser,check-client-review-browser`: 6/6). tsc and full eslint are clean; `npm test` 185/185 (this worktree has no own `node_modules`, and 6 harnesses read `./node_modules/...` by path, so they ran with a temporary junction to the main checkout's).
Files: `src/components/client-review-page.tsx`, `scripts/check-review-draft-restore-browser.cjs` (new), this file. Follow-up for the e2e owner (not done here): the funnel could wait for the page to be live before typing. The app now keeps text typed before hydration, so this is about test speed, not correctness.

### Claude — agency stage 1: common execution contract, local handback, 23 October 2026 (committed on `feature/agency-ops`; not pushed, merged or deployed)
Worktree `.claude/worktrees/agency-ops`, branch `feature/agency-ops` from `feature/agency-workflow` f884f8b (node_modules is a junction; never `--force` remove). Commits `06a8963` (migration + SQL checks), `c9a7a43` (TypeScript + harness), plus this handoff. 0 AI spend, 0 cloud spend; no production, flag, credential or billing change.

**Migration `20261023200000_agency_operation_contract.sql`** (additive; lock order operation → run → wallet → artifact unchanged):
- kinds `ingest`, `research`, `image_edit`, `independent_review`, `data_report` on operations, runs and pending kinds; an agency run must match its operation's kind; builder kinds behave exactly as before;
- `agency_task_scope`: workspace, project and client come from the artifact (never the browser); every input (source / asset / artifact version + sha256) must be in that workspace and in the artifact's project or another project of the same client; bound inside `ai_operation_create`, re-checked by `ai_operation_begin` before each new step (a changed input gets no hold);
- finish outcome `result`: typed data stored on the run (≤ 256 KB), fingerprint replay, same settlement, no project write; agency kinds cannot write plans; `ai_operation_recover` records a data result without writing or charging; late Stop after a `result` is a no-op;
- `ai_operation_heartbeat` (fenced): durable progress, Stop signal, lease renewal bounded by the operation expiry, 60 minutes from the run start and, for paying runs, the hold's fixed 15-minute lifetime (billing policy, unchanged — so in practice only exempt runs renew; paid long work uses sequential steps);
- `ai_operation_progress`: recorded state plus customer-safe call counts (in flight / uncertain / settled), no provider or model names;
- new entry points are service-only (PUBLIC execute revoked).
**Applied ONLY to the local isolated stack** (`supabase_db_makeborne-release-auth`, 127.0.0.1:55438) with `psql -1`, after a rolled-back dry run. Catalog proof: the widened `ai_operations_kind_check`, the 7 new functions and the run columns `heartbeat_at, progress, lease_renewals` are present. No cloud project touched; no `supabase db push`.

**TypeScript** (`src/lib/agency/*`, additive helpers appended to `operation-server.ts`, kind enums widened in `operation-contract.ts`, `RunSpend.extendDeadline`):
- `task.ts`: strict envelope (refuses workspace/client/project/actor/model fields), qualified settings registry — **none qualified**, so every kind is refused until a recorded live qualification — per-kind tools and credit ceilings, expected-revision check, provider ceiling = credits × rate, customer-safe view;
- `loop.ts`: one bounded tool loop for lead and specialists; tools only if permitted and schema-valid, never after Stop; draft tools re-check Stop durably (heartbeat) first; typed result through `submit_result`; no automatic retry;
- `specialist.ts`: child view of the parent RunSpend (cap ≤ share of parent remaining; every attempt through the same gate/run/fence/hold), granted tools only, bounded context, Zod-validated result, cannot publish or write;
- `model-port.ts`: `budgetedStream`, retries 0, no fallbacks (an uncertain attempt ends the step; customer pays confirmed only);
- `run.ts`: begin or replay a step (duplicates return the stored outcome, no call), cancellation watch + heartbeat, specialists only for standard-scale kinds that allow them, one finish with an explicit outcome (`result` / `saved` with expected-version CAS / `incomplete` / `failed` → `cancelled` after Stop), read-back after an ambiguous finish, uncertain attempt ids in the run detail, truthful Stop text ("A step that was already running will finish or time out; you won't be charged beyond its confirmed cost."), reconnect reads only. Flag `MAKEBORNE_AGENCY_TASKS=on` (off; not set anywhere). No routes or UI.

**Checks:** `npm test` 186/186 (new `src/lib/agency/check-agency.cjs` 22/22: envelope trust boundary, unqualified/unpriced settings, tools/ceilings, cross-workspace, stale revision, happy path, duplicate request, concurrent steps, invalid tool arguments, typed result, uncertain usage, no-retry port, unpriced reply, crash/retry allowance, expired hold, Stop mid-call, Stop with call in flight + reconnect, durable draft check, heartbeat renewal, specialist caps/permissions/shared allowance, ambiguous finish, model-routing compatibility). `tsc --noEmit` clean, eslint clean on changed files. Local SQL: `check-agency-operations.sql` A–L PASS; `check-agency-operations-concurrency.sql` (dblink, `-v allow_fixture_commit=on`) races 1–3 PASS + cleanup verified; regressions `check-ai-operations`, `check-ai-operation-cancellation`, `check-plan-approval-idempotency`, `check-usage-history` PASS on the migrated schema (the first two need their 10,000,000 fixture limit raised in-transaction because the local stack already holds 26 M microUSD of today's provider ledger rows). `check-billing-ux.sql` not run (requires a disposable rehearsal DB).

**Not done:** no qualified model setting (needs a live qualification per kind and an approved budget); no routes/UI; no paid tools (a paid tool must reserve through `ToolContext.spend`); image inputs are described, not sent; wallet-contention race for non-exempt users not added (covered by the existing wallet concurrency checks, needs membership fixtures); renewal for paying runs is impossible without a hold-lifetime policy change (owner/Codex decision, not made here).

**Integration with feature/model-routing:** `run-spend.ts` and `operation-server.ts` merge cleanly (`git merge-tree`; the only conflict, `live-builder.tsx`, already exists between main and that branch). Settlement uses `chargeableMicrousd(spend)`, which picks up routing's `chargeableMicrousd` (confirmed − absorbed) when present; no separate absorbed accounting was added. `QUALIFIED_ROUTES` and `AGENCY_SETTINGS` are separate registries today; unify them when routing qualifies cells.

Ingest agent: untouched `src/lib/ingest/*`, `attachments.ts`, `source-readiness.ts`; extraction records can join `agency_task_scope` as a new input kind later. Codex: please review the migration (scope function, finish/recover branches, heartbeat bounds). Acknowledgment not assumed.

### Claude — agency stage 2: common source-ingestion pipeline, local handback, 23 October 2026 (committed on `feature/agency-ingest`; not pushed, merged or deployed)
Worktree `.claude/worktrees/agency-ingest`, branch `feature/agency-ingest` from `feature/agency-workflow` f884f8b (node_modules is a junction to the primary's; never `--force` remove). Commits: `583974d` (pipeline, routes, UI, migration file, checks) plus this handoff. 0 AI spend, 0 cloud spend; no production, flag, credential, billing, payment, publishing, auth, refund or access-policy change. No migration applied anywhere.

**Dependencies: none added.** DOCX/PPTX/XLSX need a ZIP reader and an XML reader; both are small purpose-built modules on Node's own `zlib` (`inflateRawSync` with `maxOutputLength`, `crc32`), so a lying ZIP header cannot make the reader inflate past the per-entry limit and the XML reader refuses DOCTYPE outright (no entity expansion, no external entities). `jszip`, SheetJS and XML-parser packages were deliberately not used (jszip has no inflate-size bound; SheetJS npm builds carry advisories). `pptxgenjs` (already a dependency) is used only inside a test to produce a real deck. `npm audit` not needed for new packages (none); package.json/package-lock unchanged.

**What exists now (`src/lib/ingest/`):**
- `parsers/*.cjs` (pure, CommonJS, bytes in → extraction object out; only `zlib`): `core.cjs` format registry (`detect`: real bytes vs declared type vs name → format, or `mime-mismatch` / `archive` / `legacy-format` / `encrypted`), `zip.cjs` (central directory only; refuses ZIP64, split, duplicate/escaping names, encrypted entries; entry-count, per-entry, total and compression-ratio limits; CRC verified), `xml.cjs`, `ooxml.cjs` (internal relationships only; `TargetMode="External"` never followed — counted and reported; macros/vbaProject, ActiveX, embedded objects reported as present-and-ignored; saved chart data read from the chart part cache), `docx.cjs` (headings from styles/outline level, lists, tables with header rows, text boxes, insertions kept / tracked deletions left out, field codes never evaluated, headers/footers/footnotes/endnotes/comments, embedded pictures), `pptx.cjs` (presentation order, title/body/notes per slide, tables, chart data, SmartArt text, hidden slides flagged, pictures with slide location), `xlsx.cjs` (A1 references, shared strings, cached values only — formulas NEVER evaluated, formula text kept as untrusted data, missing cached result → partial; number formats → date/percent/currency, 1900 leap-bug and 1904 system; hidden sheets/rows flagged; external workbook links and pivots reported), `csv.cjs` (BOM UTF-8/UTF-16LE/BE, else strict UTF-8, else Windows-1252 with a warning; delimiter sniffing; RFC 4180; cells starting `= + - @` kept verbatim and flagged `formulaLike`), `pdf.cjs` (page count from the page tree, text-layer detection, encryption, scripts/actions/attachments reported; streams inflated under limits; non-text streams skipped), `cells.cjs` (numbers/units/currency/dates recognised only when unambiguous; the original text is always kept; `03/04/2026` and `1,234` stay text).
- `parse-worker.cjs` + `worker-host.ts`: one short-lived `worker_threads` worker per file, `env: {}`, `resourceLimits` (old 384 MB / young 48 MB / stack 4 MB / code 32 MB), 25 s wall clock, bytes passed as a transferred copy, ≤ 2 parsers per instance with a 16-deep queue (else busy). After the parsers load, the worker disables module loading and deletes `fetch`/`WebSocket`/etc. Outcomes: every file outcome is a record (complete, partial, failed incl. `timeout`/`memory`, unsupported, encrypted); cancellation rejects with `IngestCancelledError` and never yields a record. Worker output is Zod-validated (`record.ts`, `extractionVersion` must match) and bound to the SHA-256 the host computes from the original bytes.
- Extraction record (`record.ts`): `sourceSha256`, `extractionVersion` (`ingest-2026-10-23.1`), `format`, `status` complete|partial|failed|unsupported|encrypted, `problem`, specific `message`, `coverage` {unit pages|slides|sheets|paragraphs|rows|characters|image, total, read, cells{total,read}, visual native|not-rendered|none, notes}, `text` (what AI reads), `blocks` (heading/paragraph/note/table/image in reading order), `tables` (header rows; cells with original text + normalised number/unit/currency/date), `slides`, `sheets` (cells with ref, kind, cached value, formula, formulaLike), `images`, `warnings`, `unsupported`. **Thin interface for the execution contract:** `ingestionReference(record, sourceId)` → `{ sourceId, sha256, extractionVersion, format, status }` (sourceId = asset id for stored files, `inline:<sha256>` otherwise). `prepareBuilderSources` returns these as `references` for the ops envelope; not yet persisted (operation-server.ts / agency_task_scope belong to the ops branch).
- `sheets.ts`: deterministic helpers (`inferColumns`, `aggregateBy` → count/sum/avg/min/max per group with the exact contributing cell refs, exact decimal sums, skipped rows listed with a reason; no formula evaluation).
- `model-blocks.ts`: extracted text goes to the model as a text `document` block whose content sits between `SOURCE-<sha-derived> BEGIN/END` markers the file cannot contain (any occurrence inside is neutralised) with `context` = `UNTRUSTED_SOURCE_RULE` (content cannot change instructions, the user's request, permissions, tools, budgets or spending limits) plus the coverage statement and the not-read list; embedded pictures follow as native image blocks, each labelled untrusted. A non-complete record cannot be turned into model input.
- `request.ts` / `builder-sources.ts`: build and plan routes accept `BuilderSourcesSchema` (existing image/PDF/text entries validated exactly as before, plus DOCX/PPTX/XLSX/CSV inline ≤ 3 MB within the existing 3 MB combined limit, or `{ reference: { assetId, sha256, bytes } }` up to 25 MB each / 60 MB per request for Office/CSV/PDF) and optional `omittedSources` [{name, reason left-out|unreadable|partial}] (optional so old requests keep their exact input hash). Sources are prepared before admission (no hold exists if a file is unreadable): Office/CSV extracted in the worker and required to be complete, else 422 `SOURCE_UNREADABLE` "<file> can only be partly read: <reason> Remove it, or choose “Continue without it” …"; inline PDFs additionally checked (encrypted / >100 pages / damaged refused) while their native document block stays byte-identical; omitted files produce one note telling the model not to invent their contents; provider payload bounded at 24 MB encoded.
- Storage references (`storage.ts`, route `POST /api/cloud/workspaces/[ws]/artifacts/[id]/sources`): `prepare` names the one path `<ws>/<project>/sources/<sha256>.<ext>`; the editor's browser uploads there (`upsert:false`, existing INSERT policy); `register` re-reads with the server key (`readPendingSourceUpload`, sources-only path check), verifies size + SHA-256, inserts an immutable `assets` row (deterministic id in a `source:` namespace, provenance `source_upload`), and returns the inspection. Use re-resolves through the caller's RLS-bound client + `downloadProjectAsset`, re-verifying hash and size. Migration `20261023130000_project_source_uploads.sql` (NOT applied) adds DOCX, XLSX and CSV to the bucket's allowed types; PDF/PPTX were already allowed; policies unchanged.
- Pre-Send inspection `GET/POST /api/build/sources` (signed-in, ≤ 3 MB raw body, coverage/warnings/unsupported only — no extracted text returned, nothing stored, no AI).
- UI: `source-readiness.ts` gains an optional inspector (no inspector or switch off = today's behaviour, verified by the existing checks); a measured `partial` state; ready Office/PDF entries show coverage (“3 of 3 slides read · 2 tables · 1 embedded picture shown to AI. Slides were not rendered as pictures…”, “2 pages, all sent to AI. Scanned: no text layer…”) and a details list (warnings + not-read content); unreadable/partial files block with “Remove” (×) or “Continue without it” (the existing Leave out, relabelled for these files), recorded as `omittedSources`. Live builder uploads files over 3 MB at Send and sends the reference; the picker offers .docx/.pptx/.xlsx/.csv only when the server switch is on.

**Switch:** `MAKEBORNE_SOURCE_INGESTION_ENABLED=true` (server-side, off by default, set nowhere). Off: new types are refused by the routes with a specific 422, the inspection route returns 503, the browser sees "not available" and every file is analysed exactly as before.

**Checks:** `src/lib/ingest/check-ingest-parsers.cjs` 16/16 (malformed ZIPs: truncated, bad CRC, duplicate, traversal, absolute, lying size, invalid XML; MIME mismatch .docx-that-is-PDF, .xlsx/.csv-that-is-HTML, .pptx-that-is-DOCX, .csv-that-is-XLSX, MIME-only claim, archives, legacy .doc; bombs: 1000× ratio, lying declared size, 2001 entries, total size, PDF stream bomb; DOCTYPE/billion-laughs/XXE refused; encrypted DOCX/XLSX/PPTX CFB, encrypted ZIP entry, encrypted PDF; external image/template/hyperlink/workbook links reported with a network trap proving no access; macros/ActiveX/embedded objects; page/slide/cell/row/text limits → partial or explicit failure with exact coverage; one damaged sheet → partial; formula never recalculated (`=1+1` cached 3 stays 3); formula-like CSV text; Arabic/CJK/Bosnian round-trip in DOCX/PPTX/XLSX/CSV UTF-8/UTF-16/1252; image-only PDF; a real pptxgenjs deck with table, notes, picture and chart). `src/lib/ingest/check-ingest-pipeline.cjs` 16/16 (worker record + hash; worker lockdown probe: fs/child_process blocked, no fetch, empty env; cancellation before/mid-parse yields no record and frees the slot; 1 ms timeout and an 8 MB heap end as clean `timeout`/`memory` failures; concurrency queue; schema refusal; untrusted boundary cannot be closed by file content, injection text stays inside; request contract; switch off = byte-identical existing blocks + refusal; partial/encrypted → 422 with both choices; storage upload verified by hash, idempotent, cross-project / RLS-invisible / changed bytes refused; readiness coverage, partial blocking, omitted reasons; large files → references; inspector failure is retryable; browser preparation parity with the route schema; deterministic aggregation; inspection route off by default, auth before reading, no content returned, 413, origin). Existing `check-source-labels.cjs` updated: `partial` now exists but only from a measured extraction (the ready-PDF note without inspection is unchanged). `npm test` 187/187; `next build --webpack` exit 0 (both new routes built; the parser files are traced into `/api/build`, `/api/build/plan`, `/api/build/sources` and the sources route — Turbopack `next build` refuses the junctioned node_modules in this worktree, an environment issue, not this change); `tsc --noEmit` clean; eslint clean on changed files. Local real-file robustness run (stats only, no content printed or kept) over ~40 Office/PDF/CSV files: all parsed without crash; a 36k-cell workbook is correctly reported partial (text over the per-file AI limit); >100-page PDFs refused explicitly.

**Limits (defaults, lower-only per call):** 2,000 ZIP entries, 40 MB per part, 120 MB expanded, ratio 200× above 256 KB; 100 PDF pages; 200 slides; 50 sheets, 200,000 cells, 100,000 rows, 2,000 columns; 200,000 characters of AI text per file (same as text files today); 12 pictures (≤ 3.75 MB, ≤ 8000 px, 15 MB total) per file; 25 MB per stored file.

**Not done / needs decisions:**
- Slide/page rendering to images is NOT performed (coverage says so: layout, colours and drawn shapes aren't seen). Add only if qualification shows visual-only slides matter; the Playwright/Chromium export path could render PPTX→PDF→PNG later.
- Production isolation: a worker thread is not an OS sandbox. Before enabling in production, run the same `parsers/*.cjs` + `parse-worker.cjs` in the existing sandbox pattern (Vercel Sandbox deny-all as in `infra/project-runtime/vercel-publication-executor.mjs`, or the Docker runtime: no network, 1 CPU, 512 MiB, watchdog) and qualify cold-start latency. The files need no build step and use only `zlib`, so they copy as-is. next.config traces them into the four routes that use them.
- Per-page PDF text-layer detection (document-level only today); `.odt/.ods/.odp`, RTF, legacy binary Office and archives remain unsupported by design; EMF/WMF/TIFF pictures inside documents are reported as not shown.
- "Continue with the readable part" is not offered (the brief allows remove or continue without); a partly readable file must be split or left out.
- `omittedSources` and the `references` list are not persisted with the operation (operation-server.ts belongs to the ops branch): ops agent please add them to `operationContext` / `agency_task_scope` inputs. They are already part of the request input hash at creation.
- Creation-home/studio (no project yet) can inspect small files but cannot upload large ones; large files there are sent from the project builder.
- Live qualification with the real model (does the untrusted wrapper hold under adversarial documents? token cost of extracted spreadsheets and pictures) is outstanding: no budget approved. Estimate: about $1–2 of Anthropic usage for a 10-file adversarial set at medium effort.
- Migration `20261023130000_project_source_uploads.sql` needs review (Codex) and an approved apply; its timestamp is before the ops migration `20261023200000`, no overlap.

### Claude — agency stage 5: image editing through the existing OpenAI image path, local handback, 23 October 2026 (committed on `feature/agency-image-edit`; not pushed, merged or deployed; feature disabled; live qualification outstanding)
Worktree `.claude/worktrees/agency-image-edit`, branch `feature/agency-image-edit` from `feature/agency-workflow` 5b92a20 (node_modules is a junction; never `--force` remove). 0 AI spend, 0 cloud spend; no migration, production, flag, credential, billing or policy change. All fixtures are generated in the harness.

**Official-doc findings (checked 2026-10-23, recorded in `src/lib/ai/image-edit-prices.ts` with URLs):** `/v1/images/edits` is supported by gpt-image-2.5-sunburst (named by OpenAI for editing precision, the API's default edit model), gpt-image-2.5-flare (everyday/fast) and gpt-image-2. Rates are text $5, image input $8 and image output $30 per 1M tokens. Cached rates do not apply to the Images API, and each partial image adds 100 output tokens. A mask must match the image's format and size (< 50 MB) and needs an alpha channel. Masking is prompt-based ("may not follow its exact shape"), and a mask adds instructions of unpublished size. Up to 16 input images are allowed. Quality is low/medium/high (+ xhigh/max on 2.5, default auto). Transparency is documented for 2.5 but only "in preview" for gpt-image-2. `input_fidelity` must be omitted on gpt-image-2. `usage` is documented "for gpt-image-1 only". **The per-image input-token rule is published only for gpt-image-1, which shuts down today.** No input-image or edit output-token count is published for any model we can call, so every edit cell is refused (`MEASURED_EDIT_TOKENS` is empty).

**Built:**
- `ai/image-edit-prices.ts`: edit evidence per model, the measured-cell table (empty), `assertSupportedImageEditRequest` and `imageEditCallBoundMicrousd` (text + every input image + mask + measured mask text + output, each ×1.5 headroom, at standard rates, ×1.1; no caching or discount), usage pricing, and the `imageEditPriceCardVersion` hash. The generic image-input refusal in `prices.ts` is unchanged (comment only).
- `ai/image-edit-call.ts`: one multipart edit (image[], mask, prompt, size, quality, background, png, n=1, `stream: false`, no partials, `maxRetries: 0`, ≤180 s). It reserves the bound through RunSpend first. A thrown non-429 response is recorded as uncertain at the bound, never retried. A malformed response has its usage recorded but is never stored. An overrun goes to `reportImageOverrun`, then `markOverrun`, then SQL emergency stop.
- `images/edit/contract.ts`: the Zod contract. It covers the asset id + sha, instruction, optional mask, ≤3 references, allowlisted size/quality/background, preserve notes, protected regions, action/step keys and the ceiling. It also holds the plan and candidate schemas, the six reference kinds and the flag `MAKEBORNE_IMAGE_EDITING=on` (off).
- `images/edit/pixels.ts` (sharp): sha + decode verification and mask validation (PNG, same dims, real alpha, 0.5–99.5% editable, ≤4 MB). Inputs are normalised to the output size (aspect > 2% refused except "extend", which gets a canvas and an auto-mask). Protected regions are composited back exactly (stored lossless) and require review. It also does deterministic resize/crop/compress/exact composite with no model call.
- `images/edit/routing.ts`: precision ops go to Sunburst only, and routine ops (restyle/variant) prefer Flare, then Sunburst, then gpt-image-2. A model also needs the operator allowlist `MAKEBORNE_IMAGE_EDIT_MODELS` (unset), edit evidence, no overrun stop and a measured cell.
- `images/edit/run.ts`: one action on the existing operation (kind `image_edit`) in two fenced steps. Step 1 is Claude via the stage-1 lead loop (2 turns, 1,200 output tokens, its own small stage cap), which produces the precise instruction or "deterministic suffices". Step 2 is one edit call that stores a NEW asset (provenance: original id+sha, instruction/request hashes, model, price card, usage, cost, operation/run ids) and finishes `result` with a candidate. Duplicate keys replay both steps without a call. Failures finish `failed` (not charged). The customer view has no provider or model names.
- `images/edit/references.ts` + `accept.ts`: acceptance is one `makeborne_save_artifact_version` with the expected version and an idempotent accept key. Only the chosen uses switch: an outline block, the style reference, a document image name everywhere, one document placement (cover / slide / person / chapter opener / nth figure), a website path everywhere, or one website URL occurrence. New website paths sit beside the original, with responsive copies registered. The manifest only grows, and the original stays untouched and listed. A candidate must be an edit of that original in that project, and review is required when flagged. Discard needs no call: the candidate stays unreferenced and immutable.
- `documents/export-server.ts loadDocumentImages` now re-hashes bytes against the registered sha256 (mismatch = left out).
- Small additive stage-1 changes: an optional `stageCap` on `beginAgencyOperation` and `stage` on `runAgencyTask` (defaults unchanged), plus a `lossless` option in `storeProjectImage`.
- Routes under `/api/cloud/workspaces/[ws]/artifacts/[id]/image-edits` (GET availability, POST generate; `estimate`, `accept`, `uses`, `deterministic`, `[operationId]` read), all 404 unless the flag is on.
- UI: `components/image-edit-panel.tsx`, shown as "Edit image" on outline image blocks in cloud-studio only when the server reports the flag on and the editor is clean. The flow is describe, then maximum credit estimate, then one version, then before/after, then choose places, then Accept/Discard.

**Checks:**
- New `src/lib/images/edit/check-image-edit.cjs`, 22/22. It covers:
  - price evidence and bound math;
  - flag, settings and route refusals before any RPC;
  - unauthorized assets (other project and workspace; SQL scope as the second line);
  - invalid masks, huge or changed inputs, unsupported settings;
  - the happy path (one call, multipart shape, new asset, original bytes and row unchanged);
  - duplicate submission and timeout-uncertain (no retry, not charged);
  - insufficient budget (empty wallet, hold below the bound);
  - malformed output, overrun with emergency stop, deterministic-suffices;
  - protected regions exact with review required, extend auto-mask, deterministic ops;
  - stale and mismatched acceptance, the website flow (source resolver, responsive srcset, GitHub snapshot, publication resolver) with old bytes after discard;
  - deck slides through `loadDocumentImages` (the PDF/PPTX input) plus tamper detection, and book figures;
  - "all uses" switches and original preservation;
  - no model names in customer copy.
- `check-agency.cjs` 22/22 after the stage-1 additions.
- `npm test` 187/187.
- `tsc --noEmit` clean; eslint clean on changed files.
- The PDF/PPTX renderers themselves were not launched: they consume the verified `loadDocumentImages` buffers by name.

**Disabled / outstanding:**
- The flag is off.
- No edit model is on the allowlist, no cell is measured and no `image_edit` setting is qualified.
- Live qualification is per `docs/release-review/image-edit-qualification-20261023.md`: 24 edit calls (Sunburst 16, Flare 6, gpt-image-2 2) + 3 end-to-end runs. The planning cap is $5.83 OpenAI + $0.36 Anthropic, and the requested allowance is $6.50 + $0.50, which needs an owner-approved ledger entry first.
- Measured edit bounds extend D8 (owner sign-off).
- A failed step's specific code (uncertain vs malformed) is not stored in the SQL result (only in run detail), so a replay shows the generic "could not be made / not charged" text.
- The UI entry point is outline image blocks only; website and document images are supported by the API, and the minimal UI for them is still to do.
- Unreferenced edit candidates rely on the existing asset cleanup.
- Codex: please review accept.ts (save path and reference rules) and the stage-1 `stageCap` addition. Acknowledgment not assumed.

### Claude — agency stage 3: approved client knowledge, local handback, 23 October 2026 (committed on `feature/agency-knowledge`; not pushed, merged or deployed)
Worktree `.claude/worktrees/agency-knowledge`, branch `feature/agency-knowledge` from `feature/agency-workflow` (fast-forwarded to 4b9aa32 = stage 1 + stage 2 ingestion). node_modules is a junction; never `--force` remove. Commits `bdd8d42` (migration + SQL suite), `345c070` (TypeScript, routes, panel, harness), plus this handoff. 0 AI spend, 0 cloud spend; no production, flag, credential, billing, auth, publishing or payment change. Fixtures are generated in the tests only.

**Migration `20261023210000_client_knowledge.sql`** (additive; new tables and functions, AFTER triggers on `sources` (update of content/approved, delete), `assets` (delete) and `projects` (update of brief, delete)):
- `client_facts`: workspace + client scope (client required; workspace-own knowledge is not in this stage); kind `observed_fact | inference | user_decision | brand_preference`; normalised key; value jsonb + text, unit, currency; `valid_from/valid_to`, `observed_at`; status `proposed | approved | rejected | superseded`; `supersedes_id/superseded_by`; `conflict_group` + uncertainty note; created_by + created_via (`user`, or `system` with `operation_id`); approved/rejected actor and time; `revision` for CAS; generated `value_identity` and full-text `search`.
- `client_fact_evidence`: `source` (sha256 of content), `asset` (sha256 of bytes = ingestion `sourceSha256`), `url` (+fetched_at +sha256), `brief`, `user` (the person's words) or `artifact_version`; extraction version; locator (page/slide/sheet+A1 cell/range/paragraph/line/char range); quote ≤ 600. Ingestion references (`ingestionReference(record, assetId)`) map to `asset` evidence via `evidenceFromIngestion`; `inline:` references are refused (not durable).
- `client_fact_revisions` (append-only), `knowledge_snapshots` (immutable: fact id + revision + evidence ids + source digests/versions/locators, operation, artifact, context sha256, epoch), `client_knowledge_state` (per-client epoch).
- Entry points (service role only; PUBLIC/anon/authenticated execute revoked): `makeborne_knowledge_propose / _review / _correct / _revoke_source / _retrieve / _snapshot / _snapshot_view`. Each re-checks the actor's CURRENT role: owner/editor write, reviewer read, removed member nothing. RLS: members read all five tables; nobody writes them directly.
- Rules in SQL: a generated artifact can only support an `inference` (refused for observed facts, alone or alongside a source); tasks (operation-bound proposals) cannot create user decisions or user statements, cannot propose after Stop, and always land as `proposed`; an observed fact needs a live source to be approved; same key + overlapping validity + different value → one conflict group, never resolved silently; a newer observation contradicting one approved fact is proposed as its replacement (`supersedes_id`), applied only on approval; approving a second contradictory value is refused (`KN409`); a per client+key advisory lock orders proposals and approvals; duplicates merge evidence.
- Retrieval: approved facts valid on the task date with live evidence, ranked by Postgres full-text (`simple` config, prefix terms validated `[a-z0-9]{2,32}`, ≤ 24 terms, ≤ 100 rows, ≤ 3 citations each); optionally unapproved OBSERVED facts with a live quoted source, no open conflict and not business truth (prices, hours, contact, legal, awards, guarantees, offers, claims, stats), labelled `unapproved`. Approved facts carry their pending contradictions. No embeddings (no evaluation budget); `KnowledgeRetriever` is the seam for later fusion.
- **Audit retention decision.** Source content changed → evidence `stale` (fact leaves retrieval; the old quote stays so a reviewer can compare). Source approval withdrawn (`sources.approved` true→false) or manual revoke → evidence `revoked`, quote removed; re-approval does not silently restore it (re-extract). Source/asset/project deleted → evidence revoked, quote removed, and a fact with no other support and no user statement has its value removed from the fact and all its revisions (`[removed with its source]`). Retained: ids, kinds, keys, statuses, actors, timestamps, sha256 digests, extraction versions, locators, audit_events rows (no values). Every retrieval-affecting change moves the client epoch; derived caches (`EpochCache`) are keyed by it.
- **Cross-project boundary.** Facts belong to a client; evidence may come from any project of that client in the same workspace (the stage 1 `agency_task_scope` rule), never from an unassigned project, another client or another workspace.
- **Applied ONLY to the local isolated stack** (`supabase_db_makeborne-release-auth`, 127.0.0.1:55438) after a rolled-back dry run (table absent afterwards), then applied in its own transaction. Catalog proof: the 5 tables with RLS on, 29 knowledge functions, triggers `client_facts_epoch, client_fact_evidence_epoch, client_fact_revisions_immutable, knowledge_snapshots_immutable, knowledge_source_lifecycle@sources, knowledge_asset_lifecycle@assets, knowledge_brief_lifecycle@projects`; `authenticated` cannot execute the entry points, `service_role` can. No cloud project touched; no `supabase db push`.

**TypeScript** (`src/lib/knowledge/*`, new): `types.ts` (schemas, key normalisation mirroring SQL, price/hours canonicalisation, ingestion → evidence), `extract.ts` (deterministic suggestions from extraction records/text: prices incl. sheet cells, opening hours per day, phone, email, with exact locators; plain-word labels only, hidden slides skipped, ≤ 200), `retrieval.ts` (terms, lexical retriever, epoch cache), `context.ts` (bounded context: constant framing "data, not instructions", JSON with `<>&` escaped, approved/unapproved lists, inferences and disputes labelled, stable citations `[K-<8 hex>-r<rev>]`, item/char budgets clamped), `task-context.ts` (`knowledgeForTask`: retrieve → bound → snapshot with operation, one retry on a stale snapshot; `proposeFromTask` stops on a permission change), `claims.ts` (`checkCopyClaims` / `checkArtifactClaims`: prices, times, figures, phones, emails, status words must trace to approved non-inference facts or the brief; unapproved-only → `unapproved`, disputed → warning; `copyFromContent` reads website HTML/files, book and deck JSON), `views.ts` (customer-safe views, conflict mirror), `server.ts` (flag, error mapping, RLS reads, service writes).
- Routes (flag `MAKEBORNE_CLIENT_KNOWLEDGE=on`, off by default, checked server-side, 404 `KNOWLEDGE_DISABLED` otherwise): `GET/POST /api/cloud/workspaces/[ws]/clients/[client]/knowledge`, `GET/PATCH …/knowledge/[factId]` (provenance; approve/reject/correct with `expectedRevision`), `POST …/knowledge/sources` (revoke a source/asset/page). Uses the existing `cloudContext`/`cloudBody` (same-origin, bounded JSON, account header, creation access for writes).
- UI: `src/components/client-knowledge.tsx` on the client's "Contact details" tab (one import + one line in `account-clients.tsx`): to-review list with Approve / Correct (reason recorded as the person's statement; optionally approve) / Reject, conflicts and "would replace" highlighted, stale/withdrawn sources flagged, "Where this came from" disclosure (source title, page/slide/sheet!cell, quote, version, extraction version, history), approved list, rejected/replaced collapsed, "Add a fact or decision". Renders nothing when the flag is off. No provider/model names.

Ingestion results → `suggestionsFromExtraction(record, { kind: "asset", refId: assetId })` → `proposeForAgencyTask(...)`.

### Claude — agency stage 6: independent review, local handback, 23 October 2026 (committed on `feature/agency-review`; not pushed, merged or deployed)
Worktree `.claude/worktrees/agency-review`, branch `feature/agency-review` from `feature/agency-workflow` 5b92a20 (node_modules is a junction; never `--force` remove). Commits `4fb3142` (code + harnesses) and the docs commit with this entry. 0 AI spend, 0 cloud spend; no migration, production, flag, credential, billing, payment, publishing, auth, refund or access-policy change. Stage 2 ingestion (4b9aa32) landed on `feature/agency-workflow` after this branch was cut; no overlap (its extraction records can become review evidence later).

**New `src/lib/review/`** (the existing in-build review `builder/review.ts`/`review-policy.ts` is untouched):
- `contract.ts`: a review is bound to `{artifactId, version, contentSha}` (sha256 of the canonical version content); strict request = approved brief + approved facts (interface `ApprovedFacts` + `ClaimsCheck`, so the knowledge branch's claims check plugs in without importing unmerged code) + rendered evidence + measured diagnostics + explicit coverage (`notInspected` is mandatory). No field for builder summaries/self-assessment; strict objects refuse one. Zod `Finding`: id, category, location (route+viewport+selector | page+chapter | slide | artifact), evidence (measured diagnostic id | image crop | exact quote), severity blocker/major/minor/nit, briefRequirement (ref + exact quote) or null, factRefs, proposedRepair, verificationMethod (check id | visual), source deterministic/model, grounding measured/grounded/unverified.
- Deterministic checks (`deterministic.ts`, `probe.ts`, `pdf-inspect.ts`, `pptx-inspect.ts`, `capture-server.ts`): websites rendered from the saved static snapshot (`reviewWebsiteHtml`) in offline, script-free Chromium at 1440×900 and 390×844 — render, broken assets, page overflow, covered text (ported `coveredText` from feature/image-led-sites layout-probe), clipped text, dead links, form labels/submit, alt text, unsupported prices/percentages; books: the actual PDF from `documentPdf` read as a file — page count, 432×648 pt MediaBox, chapter coverage from the PDF outline, empty pages and runt last pages from per-page glyph counts; decks: the actual PPTX package — slide count, native editable charts (`c:chart` part with a real plot and embedded workbook), speaker notes, plus text that overflows its frame in the rendered slides. Every check reports passed/failed/not_checked (site-verifier concept); not_checked items are added to coverage.
- `validate.ts`: refusal / truncation / missing / malformed answer → `review_unavailable` (never `acceptable`), deterministic findings still attached. Schema-valid ≠ correct: evidence ids, crops, quotes, brief quotes, fact ids and routes/pages/slides must resolve against the request, factual findings must cite facts or the brief and are checked by the claims check (a value the facts support is contradicted) — otherwise `unverified` (shown, never repaired, never changes status). Whole-artifact redesign findings are capped at minor. Status: acceptable / changes_needed / review_unavailable.
- `loop.ts`: review → repair (only grounded blocker/major, with "change nothing else" instructions for the builder's existing repair path) → diff guard (`guard.ts`: only named website sections / slides / chapters may change; :root tokens, typefaces, logo and deck/book theme must not) → targeted re-verification of exactly those findings → stop. Default 1 round, clamp ≤ 2; each review/re-check spends through `childSpend` of the step's RunSpend and the loop stops when the allowance is short; a review applies only to its own revision (`REVIEW_STALE`); unresolved/guard/repair failure → `incomplete` with the findings, never complete.
- `operation.ts`: kind `independent_review` on stage 1 `runAgencyTask` (one step, `result` outcome, grounded review stored on the run, replay makes no call, `readIndependentReview` flags stale). Two additive hooks in `agency/run.ts`/`loop.ts`: lead `content` blocks (labelled images) and `finalize` (a throwing finalizer ends the step failed/uncharged).
- `cross-provider.ts`: interface only; `MAKEBORNE_CROSS_PROVIDER_REVIEW` off by default and refused in `REVIEWER_REGISTRY` while unqualified even when on.
- API `src/app/api/cloud/workspaces/[workspaceId]/artifacts/[artifactId]/independent-review/route.ts` behind `MAKEBORNE_INDEPENDENT_REVIEW=on` (404 when off; not set anywhere): GET subject / recorded review (stale flag) / repair instructions (409 when stale); POST reviews the current version. With agency tasks off or nothing qualified (today) it returns a deterministic-only result, `review_unavailable`. No UI (API only). `export-server.ts`: `dataUrls` exported (no behaviour change).

**Checks:** `npm test` 188/188 (new `check-review.cjs` 16/16, `check-review-render.cjs` 5/5 with real Chromium, PDF and PPTX; skips cleanly if Chromium is absent). Covered: no builder summary reaches the reviewer; claims normalisation; refusal/truncation/malformed/no-result; grounding and downgrades; acceptable fixtures produce no blocker/major and no repair; seeded broken asset, clipped and covered text, unsupported price, dead link, unlabelled field, phone-only overflow; book missing chapter, wrong page size, blank and runt pages, unsupported price; deck chart without its part, stripped notes, clipped text; repair fixes and is re-verified; a non-fixing repair → incomplete; a rebranding/out-of-scope repair is rejected by the guard; stale refusal; allowance; operation path, replay and reconnect. `tsc --noEmit` clean; eslint clean on changed files.

**Coverage limits (also returned in every result):** scripts, motion, hover, focus, menus and form sending are not inspected; project images on websites are not fetched by the server capture yet (reported not checked — needs an asset resolver over the project's stored assets); no PDF rasteriser (page images are print-layout slices; page metrics come from the PDF); PowerPoint's own rendering is not inspected; claims are not checked until approved facts exist (`NO_APPROVED_FACTS`).

**Remains disabled / outstanding:** both flags off; `independent_review` not in any `qualifiedKinds`; cross-provider unimplemented and unqualified; no UI. Policy question for the owner: a review step that ends `incomplete` (no result after max turns) is charged confirmed usage under stage 1 policy, although the customer gets `review_unavailable` — refusals, truncation and malformed answers are `failed` and uncharged. Live qualification plan and estimates: `docs/release-review/independent-review-qualification-20261023.md` (default reviewer 12 fixtures + 6 re-checks ≈ $2.5, cap $5; blind comparison 20 pairs ≈ $5–9, cap $12; needs an OpenAI text price-card entry first). Codex: review the two stage 1 hooks and the route's trust boundary. Acknowledgment not assumed.

**Migration `20261024010000_client_research.sql`** (additive; two new tables + service-only entry points; no existing object changed):

- `client_research_briefs`: one brief per action key (a replay returns it unchanged; a different request or another actor on the key → PT409); the optional anchor artifact must be in a project of this client; an assisted brief must name its own `research` operation; digest computed in SQL; audited (`research.brief_saved`).

- **Applied ONLY to the local isolated stack** (`supabase_db_makeborne-release-auth`, 127.0.0.1:55438): rolled-back dry run (tables absent afterwards), then applied in its own transaction. Catalog proof: both tables with RLS on, 9 research functions, `authenticated` cannot execute `makeborne_research_page_put`, `service_role` can. No cloud project, no `supabase db push`.

**TypeScript (`src/lib/research/`, new):**
- `contract.ts`: request, model answer, stored brief (Zod, server-validated), actions; source ids C1 (client record), K-… (approved knowledge), D1–D5 (ingestion records), N1 (notes), P1–P20 (pages).
- `page.ts`: pure extraction reusing `readSiteContent`/`auditSite` (leads/redesign.ts); hidden elements removed depth-matched; sentences addressed to AI tools removed and counted; visitor signals (https, phone layout, form, tap-to-call, footer year, theme colour, fonts, logo) as one citable line; robots.txt parser (own group over `*`, longest match, Allow wins ties).
- `site.ts`: bounded reader on the EXISTING guard rails — every fetch is `fetchPublic` (brand/server.ts) → `publicTarget` per hop, `pinnedFetcher` byte cap, 6 s, plus a request deadline signal. Defaults 6 pages / 2.4 MB / 25 s (hard caps 10 / 4 MB / 45 s); home always; same-site pages via `pagesToRead` only when saved knowledge + documents don't already cover the offering; supplied competitors home page only; robots honoured; social networks not read; cache reuse while valid, refetch after TTL (72 h pages, 1 h failures, 24 h robots), stale copy labelled when a refetch fails; leftovers listed as not read (page/byte/time budget).
- `material.ts`: sources + identity check (a document naming another site, an off-site home redirect, a competitor that is the client's own site → kept apart, never merged; home page not showing the name → unverified); model content with each source inside a sha-derived boundary it cannot close and an untrusted-content rule; total source text ≤ 120,000 characters per step.
- `validate.ts`: grounding for any writer — a citation must resolve to a non-excluded source containing the quote; uncited/misquoted facts → inference; facts citing only kept-apart sources removed; revenue/followers/demographics survive only as a fact whose own quote states the same term and figures; private-profile/messages/analytics claims always removed (listed without repeating the invented figure); competitors must be read competitor pages; recommendations must name website/ebook/deck/other with a guard-clean business reason.
- `deterministic.ts`: the brief without AI (quotes only), same validation. `research.ts`: replay by action key → knowledge first → documents → bounded site read → assisted step via `runAgencyTask` (kind `research`, no tools, material as content, `finalize` = grounded brief stored on the run) or deterministic → stored brief. `actions.ts`: Save to client = PROPOSED facts only (url evidence with fetched_at + sha256 + quote; asset evidence with sha256 + extraction version; corrections and notes as the person's statement; operation-bound when assisted; stops on a permission change); Use in proposal / Create a plan = brief text for the existing "Create for this client" creation flow (deck for proposals; website/book/presentation by recommendation; "other" refused).
- `server.ts` + routes `GET/POST /api/cloud/workspaces/[ws]/clients/[client]/research` and `POST …/research/[briefId]` (`save` | `proposal` | `plan`; plan asks first when any of the client's projects has a saved plan and never writes `artifact_plans`). Flag `MAKEBORNE_CLIENT_RESEARCH=on` (off; 404 before any read). Assisted writing additionally needs `MAKEBORNE_AGENCY_TASKS=on` and a qualified setting (none) — otherwise the deterministic brief, no charge. Save to client needs `MAKEBORNE_CLIENT_KNOWLEDGE=on`; documents need `MAKEBORNE_SOURCE_INGESTION_ENABLED=true` and a chosen client project.
- UI: `src/components/client-research.tsx` ("Understand this client" under the client page heading, editors only; URL, project, competitor links, notes, documents → progress → brief with Fact/Inference chips, quotes, source links and read dates, stale labels, identity warnings, questions, recommendation choice, "What was read, and what wasn't" → Save to client / Use in proposal / Create a plan, with the existing-plan confirmation). Small shared edits: `account-clients.tsx` (import + one line; optional `prefill.kind`), `studio.tsx` (`setCreating(prefill?.kind ?? "website")`). No provider/model names in customer copy.

**Checks:** new `src/lib/research/check-research.cjs` 17/17 (page extraction/hidden/injection; robots; deterministic brief on the fixture site with every fact citing url + fetched_at + quote; knowledge/documents read first → home only; citation support/downgrade/kept-apart/competitor rules; no invented revenue/followers/demographics/private access; stale cache TTL refetch + stale label; tenant isolation incl. permission change; inaccessible: timeout, 403, sign-in page, 401, robots (never requested), oversized, redirect to a private address (never connected), too many redirects, social profile, not found, and the real guard refusing 127.0.0.1/10.x/localhost/::1/credentials/ports with no fixture fetcher; conflicting identities; malicious content + boundary + no tools + material bound; search exhaustion + page/byte budgets; repeated requests without AI (zero reads) and assisted (one run, one model call, one finish, one charge; a lost brief row restored from the run without a call); Save to client proposals only; proposal/plan handoffs + existing-plan detection; boundaries/flag/no outreach/no model names/SQL parity). `npm test` 193/193; `tsc --noEmit` clean; eslint clean on changed files; `next build --webpack` exit 0 (both research routes built). **Built (all deterministic):**
- A website check of a prospect's or client's public site, scored 0–100 for opportunity:
  - It reuses the client-research reader, so robots.txt, the guard rails and the budgets apply.
  - Every finding cites url, checked-at, sha256 and a quote or measurement.
  - An unreadable site gets no score.
- A transparent fit score.
- "Add as client with this check": do-not-contact and duplicate refusals first, then an idempotent create. Facts are created as PROPOSED knowledge only.
- Duplicate suggestions with "Not the same". Nothing is merged.
- Needs attention: overdue, no next step, or stalled per stage.
- Next-step text with compare-and-set.
- A do-not-contact list.
- A unified client timeline.
- An honest first-email draft:
  - It uses only measured findings and approved facts, the sender's identity and an opt-out line.
  - It is copy-only and never sent.
  - The AI polish seam is faked, unqualified and refused.

**UI:**
- A client-list panel.
- A client Outreach-tab block.
- "Check website" under each Lead finder result.

Shared-file edits are small:
- `account-clients.tsx`: 2 imports + 2 mount lines.
- `lead-finder.tsx`: an optional `check` slot.
- `check-lead-finder.cjs`: one stub.

**Flag:** `MAKEBORNE_CRM_RESEARCH=on`, off and set nowhere. While it is off, routes answer 404 and panels render nothing.

**Migration** `20261025300000_crm_research.sql`:
- Additive. It adds `crm_site_checks`, `crm_next_steps` (+ `makeborne_crm_next_step_set`), `crm_suppressions`, `crm_duplicate_dismissals` and the service-only `makeborne_crm_purge_expired_checks`.
- RLS: members read; owners and editors write, as themselves.
- It was applied ONLY to the local isolated stack `supabase_db_makeborne-release-auth`, after a rolled-back dry run. There was no `db push`.

**Tests:**
- New offline harness 13/13, using a local 127.0.0.1 fixture server; no public network.
- SQL `supabase/check-crm-research.sql` A–G PASS. This includes the real knowledge entry point and the real CRM write-protocol triggers.
- Regressions pass: knowledge, research, client-directory, agency-operations and brand-kits.
- Mounted browser check `scripts/check-crm-research-browser.cjs` 9/9 in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.
- `npm test` 196/196; tsc clean; full eslint clean; `next build --webpack` exit 0.

**Needs Codex (owner of mail and the Lead-finder route):**
- **Enforce the do-not-contact list in the mail send path and in sequences.** Call `suppressionFor` on `crm_suppressions` in `mail/engine.ts` `sendEmail` and at enroll and step time; refuse with 409 `CONTACT_SUPPRESSED`.
- **Optionally, a no-AI Lead finder path** when chat is off. This is an owner pricing decision: do delivered leads still cost 0.2 credits each?
- **Review the migration and both routes' trust boundary.**
- **Schedule the purge if this is activated.**

**Needs the owner:**
- **Flag activation.** Nothing is qualified for production.
- **Optional AI polish qualification:** about 40 calls, under $0.50 expected, $1 cap.
- **Web search stays refused,** because the result-token ceiling is still the blocker.

Acknowledgment is not assumed.
